**`EV-5b` — Make the category gate SENSITIVE at n≈67: a paired McNemar delta and a support-floored macro-F1**

- *files:* `eval/scoring.py`, `tests/test_scoring.py`
- *changes:* ⚠ **NEW STEP — nobody scoped this, and without it D1's pool clears `min_n` without making the gate mean anything. At n≈67 the standard error on an accuracy near 0.80 is `sqrt(0.8·0.2/67) ≈ 0.049`, so `regression_gate`'s `epsilon = 0.02` sits well INSIDE the noise floor: the unpaired gate would both fail on noise and miss real regressions. v6 and v7 score the SAME rows, so the correct statistic is paired.** **(a)** `paired_facet_deltas(current_preds, baseline_preds, truth, facets=('category','level')) -> dict[str, dict]`. Align all three by `job_listing_id` (**rows missing from any side are skipped and COUNTED as `skipped`**). Per facet return `{'b','c','net','n_paired','p_value'}` where **b = baseline right & current wrong, c = baseline wrong & current right**, and `p_value` is the **EXACT two-sided binomial test** on `(min(b,c), b+c, 0.5)` computed with `math.comb` — ⚠ **stdlib only, no scipy/numpy (verified absent from pyproject.toml), preserving the module docstring's "pure, no network, fully unit-testable" contract.** `p_value` is None when `b+c == 0`. **(b)** `_macro_f1(min_support: int = 1)` — ⚠ **the DEFAULT stays 1 so `category_f1_macro` and the committed `baseline.json` value 0.679 are byte-identical and `test_macro_f1_skips_zero_support_classes` (test_scoring.py:95) passes unchanged.** `to_dict()` emits a SECOND key `category_f1_macro_supported` computed at `PARAM_MIN_CLASS_SUPPORT` plus `category_f1_macro_excluded_classes`. ⚠ **WHY: measured on the seed, `growth` and `hardware_engineer` have exactly 2 rows each and BOTH are already human, with zero draft rows available to draw more — so on the human pool each carries a full 1/6 of a GATED facet off two rows, where one flip moves the macro by 0.083, four times the gate epsilon.** **(c)** ⚠ **Both are pure functions only. `regression_gate` is NOT changed to call them — `EV-12`'s protocol invokes them as explicit gate arms G1b and G1c, which keeps `regression_gate`'s signature and default behaviour frozen.**
- *dependsOn:* `["EV-5"]`
- *tests:* A fixture where v7 fixes 5 rows and breaks 1 yields b=1, c=5, net=4 and p < 0.15; a symmetric 3/3 fixture yields net=0 and p == 1.0; ⚠ **b=2, c=0 yields p == 0.5 (the case `PARAM_PAIRED_MIN_NET` exists to let through)**; rows absent from either prediction file are skipped and counted; ⚠ **`_macro_f1()` with no argument returns exactly the pre-change value on the existing fixture**; `category_f1_macro_supported` at min_support=5 drops a 2-row class and names it in `category_f1_macro_excluded_classes`.
- *acceptance:* Green; ⚠ **`paired_facet_deltas` over the committed v6 predictions AGAINST THEMSELVES returns b=0, c=0, net=0, p_value=None for every facet (the identity check).** · *risk:* low · *effort:* S

**`EV-8` — `cli review-build --facet …` — corroborator, tiers, the seeded stratified audit draw, and the review artifact**

- *files:* `eval/prelabel.py`, `cli.py`, `golden/review/category-review.json`, `tests/test_prelabel.py`
- *changes:* **FACET-GENERIC.** For each pre-labelled row, compare the pre-label against a **CORROBORATOR chosen per facet**: `--facet subcategory` → `subcategory_rules.rule_label` (EV-4) — ⚠ **an LLM and a regex fail independently, which is exactly what makes agreement evidence.** `--facet category` → the row's existing `draft` category label, and ⚠ **THE CORRELATION MUST BE DECLARED: the 252 draft labels were produced by the original claude-code workflow (commit `3d6072d`, classify default haiku + judge sonnet, enrich_config.py:21/42), so a claude-code pre-labeller agreeing with them is same-FAMILY evidence. The header therefore records `corroborator_independent: false` for the category facet, and `EV-2b` runs with promotion DISABLED as a consequence.** **TIERS:** **A** = corroborator returns nothing (untypable) → **ALL to human, the hardest and most valuable rows**. **B** = corroborator produced a value AND it disagrees with the pre-label → **ALL to human**. **C** = agreement → candidate auto-promotion, EXCEPT an audit sample drawn under the committed seed, ⚠ **STRATIFIED by the corroborator's primary value with a guaranteed floor of one slot per value present in C (an unstratified sample of a pool dominated by backend/infrastructure_platform would audit almost none of the rare slugs).** `--audit-rate` defaults to **0.15 for `subcategory` and 1.0 for `category`** — ⚠ **1.0 == every drawn row reviewed == promotion is moot, which is the honest consequence of `corroborator_independent: false`.** WRITES `golden/review/<facet>-review.json` with a full provenance header (facet, prelabel_engine, prelabel_model, under_test, seed, taxonomy_version, corroborator, corroborator_independent, promote_agreements, audit_rate, tier_counts, generated_at) and per-row `{…, tier, corroborator_value, prelabel, prelabel_confidence, prelabel_reasoning, decision: null, decision_note: null}`. `decision` is what the human fills: **a single slug for category, an ORDERED list of ≤2 for subcategory.** Non-audited tier-C rows go to `golden/review/<facet>-agreements.json`. ⚠ **`golden/review/` is matched by no .gitignore rule (verified), so these artifacts are committable provenance.** Optional `cli review-render --facet <f>` emits one self-contained static HTML — radio buttons for category, 15 ordered checkboxes for subcategory, a copyable JSON blob — **so ~25-30 s/row is realistic; hand-editing the JSON is the fallback.**
- *dependsOn:* `["EV-7","EV-4"]`
- *tests:* Tier assignment on a crafted fixture; ⚠ **the audit sample is exactly `ceil(audit_rate * |C|)`, stratified, and byte-identical across two runs with the same seed**; a value appearing once in C gets at least one audit slot; a row can never land in two tiers; `--facet category` stamps `corroborator_independent: false` and `audit_rate: 1.0` by default; `--facet subcategory` stamps `true` and `0.15`.
- *acceptance:* `|review rows| == |A| + |B| + ceil(audit_rate*|C|)`, and re-running with the same seed produces a **byte-identical** file. · *risk:* low · *effort:* M

**`EV-9` — `cli review-apply --facet …` — mark rows human, write the audit verdict, enforce the promotion switch**

- *files:* `eval/prelabel.py`, `cli.py`, `golden/seed/golden.json`, `golden/seed/<facet>_audit.json`, `tests/test_prelabel.py`
- *changes:* **FACET-GENERIC.** `--facet category`: write `row['category'] = decision`, `row['label_source'] = 'human'`, `row['category_prelabel']`, `row['category_prelabel_model']`. ⚠ **Level, tags and locations are UNTOUCHED — this pass decides one facet.** `--facet subcategory`: write `row['subcategories'] = decision` (ordered, ≤2), `gold_subcategory_provenance = 'human'` for tiers A/B and `'human_audited'` for the tier-C audit rows, plus the prelabel model. Also assign `split`: ⚠ **any row destined to become a v7 SKILL.md few-shot is `split='train'` and is EXCLUDED from the gate pool** (there is no holdout today and the v6 few-shots were drawn from golden rows). **AUDIT VERDICT, computed from the audit rows ONLY:** `error_rate = |{audited rows where the human decision differs from the prelabel}| / |audited|`, written to `golden/seed/<facet>_audit.json` with the full header (facet, seed, sampled, reviewed, errors, error_rate, threshold, passed, promote_agreements, prelabel_model, under_test, corroborator, corroborator_independent, generated_at). ⚠ **PROMOTION SWITCH:** when `promote_agreements` is true AND `error_rate <= threshold`, the non-audited tier-C rows are promoted to `'prelabel_agreed'`, which `load_truth(facet=…)` then admits. **When it is false (the category pass) or the threshold is exceeded, `passed` is written HONESTLY, NOTHING is promoted, the command prints the remaining worklist, and `load_truth` drops every `prelabel_agreed` row. This is the "we learned something cheaply" branch made operational instead of aspirational — it is the only thing between a pre-labelled pool and a gate that blesses a model's blind spots.** Applying twice is idempotent. **Contract rows are never promoted.**
- *dependsOn:* `["EV-8"]`
- *tests:* An audit at or below the threshold with `promote_agreements: true` promotes C, one above promotes none, ⚠ **and `promote_agreements: false` promotes none regardless of the error rate**; ⚠ **`load_truth(facet=…)` respects the audit file INCLUDING "missing file == not passed"**; applying twice is idempotent; contract rows are never promoted; a `split='train'` row is excluded from the gate pool; `--facet category` never writes a `subcategories` key.
- *acceptance:* On a fixture, `review-apply --facet category` moves exactly the decided rows to `label_source='human'` and writes `category_audit.json` with a real `error_rate`; **a second identical run changes no bytes.** · *risk:* medium · *effort:* M

**`EV-10` — `report.py`: the subcategory headline, provenance stamps, `--facet`, `--gate-group`, and the two independence rails**

- *files:* `eval/report.py`, `cli.py`, `tests/test_report.py`
- *changes:* **DEPENDENCY CHANGE:** dropped the draft's `ENR-CLS-3` edge **so the weak-rule baseline (`EV-11`) is not pushed behind `ENR-TAX-2`**; the `enrich-eval.md` key-list edit moves to `EV-12`, which already edits that file and already sits after `ENR-CLS-3`. `_headline` (**:52-73**) gains `subcategory [SWE only] primary_ne= set_f1= macro_f1= coverage= leak= card= excl= n_sub=`. ⚠ **Print `n_sub` EXPLICITLY: a reader who sees only an accuracy number on a thin pool will over-trust it.** Beside the `gold_quality` stamp (**:136-137**) add `subcategory_gold_quality` (the modal provenance across the admitted subcategory pool — **it carries different provenance than the category pool**), `subcategory_n`, `subcategory_prelabel_model`, and `subcategory_audit_passed`. `run_eval` gains `facet` and `gate_groups`. Add `--facet {all,subcategory}` and repeatable `--gate-group` to the `eval` parser (**cli.py:1462-1477**), ⚠ **so a subcategory-only scorecard can be produced without polluting `scorecard.json` — which is the exact filename `metrics.py:_fresh_scorecard` (:81) ships to JVN.** ⚠ **TWO RAILS when the subcategory group is gated:** **(1) INDEPENDENCE** — fail with `subcategory gate refused: prelabel model '<m>' == model under test '<m>' (independence rule a)` when the stamped `subcategory_prelabel_model` matches the run's engine/model; **(2) AUDIT** — fail with `subcategory gate refused: agreement audit not passed` when `subcategory_audit_passed` is not true and any `prelabel_agreed` row is in the pool. Threading the live ceiling through would silently invalidate `baseline.json`'s committed 0.4937/0.1702. Instead add `--conf-threshold` defaulting to 0.80 (comparability preserved) and emit a SECOND key `calibration_error_rate_below_at_ceiling` computed at 0.85, documenting both.**

**`EV-2b` — THE CATEGORY POOL RUN (D1): take the human pool from 21 to ≥55 BEFORE SKILL.md is touched**

- *files:* `golden/review/category-prelabel.json`, `golden/review/category-review.json`, `golden/seed/golden.json`, `golden/seed/category_audit.json`, `docs/EVAL-GOLD-POOL.md`
- *changes:* ⚠ **NEW STEP, and the id `ENR-TAX-2` depends on. This is D1 executed, and the whole epic's gate hangs on it.** Today `regression_gate` sets `n = current.category.n` (**verified scoring.py:418**) and refuses below `GATE_MIN_N = 50` (**:402**, refusal :419-424), while `load_truth(human_only=True)` yields exactly **21** (verified: golden.json is 273 rows, 252 draft / 21 human). ⚠ **The only alternative, `--allow-draft`, is `return 0 if allow_draft else 1` (report.py:178) with argparse help "never gates, always exits 0" — so §08 phase 5's stop condition cannot fire under ANY configuration. This step removes that.** **RUN, in order:** (1) `cli prelabel --facet category --engine claude-code --model opus --under-test ollama:qwen3:32b --draw <PARAM_CAT_DRAW_N> --seed 20260820`; (2) `cli review-build --facet category` (corroborator = the seed's draft label, `promote_agreements: false`, `audit_rate: 1.0` — **every drawn row is human-decided, because the pre-labeller and the draft labels are same-family**); (3) **Brendan decides every row** via `cli review-render --facet category`; (4) `cli review-apply --facet category`. ⚠ **STOP CONDITION, checkable and not aspirational: `len(load_truth(human_only=True)) >= 55` AND every drawn row has a non-null `decision`. 55, not 50, so one later category-moving correction cannot silently drop the pool back under `GATE_MIN_N` and re-inert the gate.** ⚠ **BYPRODUCT THAT IS ACTUALLY THE POINT for the next pass: `category_audit.json`'s `error_rate` is `PARAM_PRELABEL_ERROR_RATE` — the FIRST measured prior for how often a claude-code/opus pre-label is wrong. `EV-9b`'s `PARAM_AUDIT_MAX` is derived from it instead of invented.** `EVAL-GOLD-POOL.md` records: the draw seed and strata, the final composition per category, the measured `error_rate`, review wall-clock, and ⚠ **THE CAVEAT — the 21 pre-existing human rows came from JVN's needs-human triage feed (docs/ENGINE-COMPARISON.md: "21 human-corrected rows pulled from prod JVN"), i.e. HARD rows, while the new draw is random, so absolute accuracy on the combined pool is NOT comparable to `baseline.json`'s n=252 draft number. **`EV-14` — Calibration and judge-volume: measure both channels, and codify the one that stays closed**

- *files:* `eval/scoring.py`, `eval/report.py`, `cli.py`, `docs/EVAL-V6-V7-PROTOCOL.md`, `tests/test_report.py`, `tests/test_cli.py`
- *changes:* The question is **what a second, finer decision does to the confidence distribution and therefore to judge volume.** **CONTROL:** the v6 **OLLAMA** confidence histogram from `EV-2` — ⚠ **and a correction the design tracks need, because the 71.8%-below-0.85 / 73.5%-among-SWE figures they quote come from `predictions.judge_off.json`, a 2026-07-08 CLAUDE-CODE artifact (252 rows), not the production engine. Building a cost model on the wrong engine's distribution is how this goes quietly wrong.** POST-v7, measure three things and emit them into the scorecard: **(a)** `conf_below_ceiling_share` — the share of rows with `confidence < judge:confidence_ceiling` (0.85, enrich_config.py:55), **the direct driver of `_needs_judge`**; **(b)** the `needs_human` rate; **(c)** `subcategory_nulled_rate`. ⚠ **CHANNEL (c) IS THE ONE NOBODY SCOPED: the separate `subcategory_confidence` field protects the 0.85 routing from subcategory uncertainty bleeding into `confidence`, but it does NOT protect the second path — `validate_subcategories` nulls a bad slug, the invalid-slug path flags `needs_human`, and `_needs_judge` (cli.py:514-527) returns True on `e["needs_human"]` REGARDLESS of confidence. So hallucinated slugs buy judge volume even with a separate confidence field.** ⚠ **CODIFY THE DECISION THAT STAYS CLOSED (this is `EV-6`'s other residue), as a comment at `_needs_judge` plus a test: do NOT add `category=='software_engineering' AND subcategories IS NULL` to the OR-chain — 126 of 273 golden rows are SWE, so it would route roughly half the corpus to the judge on its own — and `[]` is a legal EVALUATED answer that must never be read as a nulled facet.** **COST TRANSLATION for the doc:** at the measured ollama judge median ~92 s/row and `enrich:per_tick_limit = 40` per in-process stage, each extra judged row is ~92 s off a lane already at ~100% duty cycle — ⚠ **and `docs/ENGINE-COMPARISON.md` §1 measured the local judge as NET-NEGATIVE on category accuracy (−5.3 pts, macro-F1 0.73→0.55, judge lift −0.053), so any design routing more rows to it amplifies a stage already known to hurt.** Note the distribution's SHAPE: **the model emits coarse round-number confidences, so a shift moves rows across the ceiling in discrete clumps — expect a step change, not a smooth one.**
- *dependsOn:* `["EV-2","EV-12"]`
- *tests:* `conf_below_ceiling_share` and `subcategory_nulled_rate` appear in `to_dict()`. ⚠ **`_needs_judge` does not consult subcategories, and a row with `subcategories == []` and confidence above the ceiling is NOT routed to the judge.**
- *acceptance:* The protocol carries a before/after table with both channels and an explicit verdict against `PARAM_CAL_DELTA` and `PARAM_NULL_RATE`; **if either is exceeded the doc names the mitigation taken** (raise `judge:confidence_ceiling` and re-measure calibration at the new threshold, or tighten the slug validator). · *risk:* medium · *effort:* M

**`BF-4` — Selector: the `backfill_scope` join and the two predicates the decision record overturned**

- *changes:* `ENR-ST-2` owns `rows_needing_subcategory` and `subcategory_backlog_counts`. **This step is the backfill's EDIT on top of them, and it exists as its own step because two of those predicates are overturned by the decision record and one predicate belongs to a table `ENR-ST-2` does not know about.** **ADD the scope join:** `rows_needing_subcategory(limit, max_attempts=3, open_only=False)` gains, when `open_only`, `AND EXISTS (SELECT 1 FROM backfill_scope s WHERE s.source_id = e.source_id AND s.job_listing_id = e.job_listing_id)`. ⚠ **`backfill_scope`, NOT `backfill_roster` — that table is deleted.** `P0-4` owns the table and the importer. ⚠ **REMOVE two predicates, with a code comment naming the decision record so a future reader does not restore them:** **(a)** `title_only = 0` — **the record overturns "those rows can never be labelled" with "title-first labels 1,081 of them; 747 remain", and the title_only SWE rows are precisely where the free rule pass (EV-4) pays**; **(b)** `clean_description IS NOT NULL AND TRIM(...) <> ''` — **the backfill prompt never reads a description (the injection boundary, enforced structurally in `BF-6`'s row projection), so requiring one is both pointless and re-excludes the same cohort.** **KEEP and document:** `i.status IN ('sent','enriched')` excludes the terminal 'failed' rows and the ~262-row 'cleaned' cohort mid-reclassification. ⚠ **Note in the comment that this EXCLUDES the poison cohort rather than being blocked by it — that is the specific way the discarded `SEL-1`'s `classify_deferred > 0` veto failed (it would have emitted an empty manifest forever, silently).** ⚠ **CLAIM SEMANTICS: there are none, deliberately.** Single-flight is `BF-9`'s `.backfill.lock`; a written row stops being NULL, so re-running the selector after each chunk advances with no OFFSET, no claim column, no checkpoint. ⚠ **This is ONLY safe because `subcategory_attempts` is bumped on failure too (`BF-8`) — otherwise a permanently-failing row is re-selected forever and the loop spins in place while reporting progress. Put that sentence in the comment above the selector and pin it with a test.** `--open-only` **REFUSES (exit 2)** when `backfill_scope` is empty or its newest `imported_at` is older than `SCOPE_MAX_AGE_HOURS = 24` — ⚠ **a silently-empty scope table would make `--open-only` a no-op that reports success; jobs also open and close overnight, so a stale scope is a wrong scope.**
- *dependsOn:* `["ENR-ST-2","P0-4"]` — ⚠ **the ambiguous `ST-2` (defined independently by BOTH the enricher and the frontend) is resolved to the enricher's store step.**
- *tests:* ⚠ **the selector INCLUDES `title_only = 1` rows AND rows with an empty `clean_description` — the two standing regressions guarding the corrections above; these fail if `ENR-ST-2`'s predicates come back**; it excludes non-SWE, already-set (**both a real array and the string `'[]'`**), `subcategory_attempts >= cap`, status 'failed' and status 'cleaned'; ordering is newest-first with NULL `first_seen_at` last; `open_only=True` returns only rows present in `backfill_scope`; **an empty scope table and a scope older than `SCOPE_MAX_AGE_HOURS` each raise/refuse rather than returning `[]`**; ⚠ **a row whose attempts are bumped on every pass eventually leaves the selector (the anti-spin test).**
- *acceptance:* Against a read-only copy of the prod DB with the migration applied: `rows_needing_subcategory(limit=100000)` returns the count of SWE rows in status sent/enriched (~11,144 as measured), and after hand-setting 50 of them to `'[]'` it returns **exactly 50 fewer with no other change**. With `backfill_scope` imported, `open_only=True` returns a count within a few percent of the 8,126 OPEN figure. · *risk:* low · *effort:* S

**`BF-6` — Echoed-pair result matching — positional repair stays OFF — plus the trusted-row projection**

- *files:* `backfill.py` (new), `tests/test_backfill.py`, `tests/test_engines.py`
- *changes:* ⚠ **REVERSES the draft's ENG-1/BF-6 proposal.** `ChunkEngine._call_with_repair` stamps identity ONLY when `len(inputs) == 1 and len(results) == 1` (**base.py:122-127**); at batch>1 nothing is stamped and each result must carry its own echoed pair. **The draft added `positional_repair_on_count_match=True`, which stamps by POSITION whenever the counts match — that assumes an order guarantee nothing provides, and a reordered response writes row A's labels onto row B. Because the written value is non-NULL, that row leaves the selector PERMANENTLY, a rerun cannot fix it, and the NULL tri-state cannot detect it. NO SUCH FLAG IS ADDED.** `ENR-PB-1`'s `ChunkEngine.subcategory` calls `_call_with_repair` with the existing three-argument signature and inherits the existing behaviour unchanged. **AUTHORED HERE, in the new module `backfill.py`:** ⚠ **`chunk_rows(rows) -> list[dict]` — the trusted-row projection. Each dict is EXACTLY `{job_listing_id, source_id, title, company, tags}` and nothing else. This is the injection boundary made STRUCTURAL rather than procedural: `claude_code.py`'s docstring (lines 8-10) reserves the sandboxed sub-agent fan-out for untrusted scraped text and limits headless `claude -p` to trusted input; a job description CANNOT leak into a `claude -p` prompt because the row handed to the builder has nowhere to put one. Raises `ValueError` if a source row carries a `clean_description`/`description_html` key that would otherwise be copied.** `match_results_to_chunk(chunk, results) -> (matched, stats)` — **the replacement for positional repair.** Build `wanted = {(source_id, job_listing_id): row}` from the chunk. For each result: **both echoed ids must be present and non-empty strings; the pair must be a member of `wanted`; the pair must not already have been matched in this chunk.** Any failure drops the result and increments `stats.echo_mismatch` — ⚠ **a duplicate echoed pair drops BOTH the duplicate and the already-matched result, because a collapsed pair is not evidence for either row.** Chunk rows with no matching result increment `stats.unanswered`. **Both counters roll into `backfill_runs.errors` AND stand alone in the ledger so the morning report can name which failure it was.** ⚠ **`stats.unanswered` and `stats.echo_mismatch` rows take a PARSE-failure disposition in `BF-9` (attempts bumped, `subcategories` left NULL) — never an empty-array write, and never a transport-error disposition.**
- *dependsOn:* `["ENR-ENG-1","ENR-ENG-3","ENR-PB-1"]` *(the `ENR-ENG-1` edge is real, not decorative: ENG-1's deliberate NON-change is what BF-6's standing regression in `test_engines.py` guards)*
- *tests:* ⚠ **THE REQUIRED REGRESSION — `test_reversed_batch_without_echoes_writes_nothing`: a chunk of 4 rows, a fake engine returning 4 result objects in REVERSE order with `source_id`/`job_listing_id` OMITTED (exactly the shape a positional-repair implementation would happily stamp); assert `record_subcategories` is never called (monkeypatched to raise), all 4 rows are still `subcategories IS NULL`, `echo_mismatch == 4`, and each row's `subcategory_attempts` went up by 1.** Companion cases: `test_reversed_order_with_correct_echoes_writes_to_the_right_rows` (**same reversal but each result carries its OWN pair — all 4 written, row A's slugs land on row A, proving pair-lookup rather than position**); `test_duplicate_echoed_pair_drops_both`; `test_echo_naming_a_row_outside_the_chunk_is_dropped_and_counted`; `test_partial_response_counts_unanswered_and_bumps_only_the_missing_rows`; ⚠ **`chunk_rows` raises on a row carrying `clean_description` and its output keys are exactly the five named.** In `tests/test_engines.py` — ⚠ **the standing negative guard `test_call_with_repair_stamps_identity_only_at_batch_one` asserts that at `len(inputs) == 3` with 3 results carrying wrong ids, NOTHING is stamped. This test fails if anyone re-adds positional stamping to base.py, which is the point of it; its docstring names this step and the reason.**
- *acceptance:* `python -c` over 10 real prod rows: ⚠ **`chunk_rows` output serialises to JSON containing none of the rows' `clean_description` substrings**; a hand-built response with two ids swapped between objects produces `echo_mismatch == 2` and **zero writes.** · *risk:* medium · *effort:* M

**`DEP-2` — Cross-repo deploy ORDER, with the failure mode of every inversion stated**

- *files:* `docs/RUNBOOK-subcategory-backfill.md`, `JVN/src/backend/CLAUDE.md`
- *changes:* Repointed from the deleted `P0-6` to `SCHEMA-6` — **what this step actually needs is the confirmation that Phase 1 is LIVE.** THIRTEEN steps, **each with its inversion failure mode:** **1** JVN Phase 1 (⚠ **inverted: `EnrichmentResultItem` has no `extra='forbid'`, so labels are DROPPED while `written: N` is returned — the worst inversion**); **2** verify Phase 1 in prod (`SCHEMA-6`, the deploy-skip trap); **3** enricher schema-only deploy, dark; ⚠ **4 D1 — raise the CATEGORY human gold pool past 50 (without it `regression_gate` refuses at n<50 and `--allow-draft` never gates, so phase 5 is decorative)**; **5** capture v6 baselines (⚠ **irreversible once SKILL.md's hash moves**); **6** taxonomy v7 with §1b kept COMPACT per D3; **7** the v6→v7 gate; **8** flip write-back; **9** canary (**which also sets the breaker thresholds**); **10** the sustained run; ⚠ **11 D2 — the throttled bulk drain into `SCHEMA-14`**; **12** JVN Phase 2 + frontend (⚠ **inverted before `RA-6`: the proxy drops the param and returns an unfiltered 200**); **13** flip the reveal flag at 90%.

**`BF-8` ⚠ — Write path: the backfill-only concerns `ENR-ST-2` does not carry (failure bump, run stamping, `sent_at` restraint)**

- *files:* `store.py`, `tests/test_store.py`
- *changes:* `ENR-ST-2` owns `record_subcategories` (the base write) and the `record_judge` facet-change detection. **This step adds the four things a backfill needs that a live classify path does not, and pins the two invariants that are easiest to break by copy-paste.**
  **1.** `record_subcategories` gains `run_id: int | None = None` and `bump_attempt: bool = True`, writing `subcategory_run_id` and `subcategory_attempts = subcategory_attempts + ?`. Nothing else changes.
  **2.** ⚠ **`record_subcategory_failure(source_id, job_listing_id)` — bump `subcategory_attempts` ONLY, leave `subcategories` NULL. This is the parse-failure / unanswered / echo-mismatch / all-slugs-invalid path, and the distinction is the highest-value line in the step: an explicit `[]` from the model is a legitimate TERMINAL answer, while a failure must stay NULL so the row is retried and, at the cap, retired visibly.**
  **3.** ⚠ **INVARIANT PINNED — `record_subcategories` MUST NOT touch `sent_at` or `inbox.status`, ever, with no `reopen` parameter at all.** The draft's `reopen=True` variant is DELETED along with the re-open drain it served. **Verified premise: 11,144 of 11,150 prod SWE rows are already `sent`; `write_back` calls `store.enriched_unsent()` with NO limit (store.py:542-559) and batches at `settings.writeback_batch_size` = 50 (config.py:22), and `_row_to_result` (writeback.py:35-67) carries the full `clean_description` — so an unconditional re-open is ~163 batches of full descriptions at Railway inside one tick.**
  **4.** ⚠ **INVARIANT PINNED — `record_subcategories` MUST NOT touch `judged`, `judge_pass`, `judge_confidence`, `judge_notes`, `judge_reasoning`, `judge_latency_ms`, `judge_model`, or any `pre_judge_*` column. A subcategory is an ADDITIVE facet, unlike `record_enrichment` (store.py:439-443) which deliberately resets all of them because a re-classification has no judge yet. Assert column by column.**
  **5.** ⚠ **`mark_sent` (store.py:561-575) additionally sets `subcategory_sent_at` — but ONLY when `subcategories IS NOT NULL` AND `enrich_config.get_writeback_send_subcategories(store)` is TRUE. The knob condition is load-bearing and the draft missed it: with the knob off `_row_to_result` omits the field (`ENR-WB-1`), so stamping `subcategory_sent_at` anyway would mark live-lane rows as pushed when nothing was ever sent, and `BF-12b`'s drain would skip them forever.**
  **6.** `record_enrichment`'s INSERT/ON CONFLICT lists (**store.py:424-458**) zero `subcategory_attempts`, `subcategory_run_id` and `subcategory_sent_at` on a re-classify.
- *dependsOn:* `["BF-1","ENR-ST-2"]` — ⚠ **the second ambiguous `ST-2` resolved.**
- *tests:* `record_subcategories` sets every field, bumps attempts, stamps `subcategory_run_id`, ⚠ **and leaves `judged`/`judge_pass`/`judge_confidence`/`judge_notes`/`judge_reasoning`/`judge_latency_ms`/`judge_model`/`pre_judge_*` byte-identical (explicit column-by-column assertion)**; ⚠ **on a row whose `sent_at` is set, `sent_at` is UNCHANGED and `inbox.status` stays 'sent' (the amplification regression) — and `record_subcategories` has no `reopen` parameter at all (`inspect.signature` assertion, so the deleted behaviour cannot creep back)**; `record_subcategory_failure` bumps attempts and leaves `subcategories` NULL; an explicit `[]` stores the string `'[]'` and is NOT re-selected; ⚠ **`mark_sent` stamps `subcategory_sent_at` with the knob ON and does NOT with the knob OFF (both directions)**; `record_enrichment` on re-classify zeroes attempts, run_id and `subcategory_sent_at`.
- *acceptance:* On a copy of prod: label 10 rows, then ⚠ **`SELECT COUNT(*) FROM enrichments WHERE sent_at IS NULL` is unchanged from before and `store.enriched_unsent()` returns the same count it did before (the amplification acceptance).** With the write-back knob off, `SELECT COUNT(*) FROM enrichments WHERE subcategory_sent_at IS NOT NULL` is 0 after a write-back cycle. · *risk:* medium · *effort:* M

**`BF-11` ⚠ — Two live-lane subcategory breakers over a rolling tick window (a `[]`-emitting tick must not close ok)**

- *files:* `metrics.py`, `cli.py`, `tests/test_metrics.py`, `tests/test_cli.py`
- *changes:* ⚠ **Decision-record blocker #2, verified in code today: `drift_suspected` (metrics.py:46-49, constants `DRIFT_MIN_CLASSIFIED = 10` at :42 and `DRIFT_NULLED_RATIO = 0.5` at :43) fires ONLY on `nulled_facets`, so a classify wave that emitted `[]` for every SWE row produces ZERO nulled facets, the tick closes `status=ok`, and JVN's verdict banner stays green. This worker has a documented history of ticks reporting `status=ok` while dead, so a green banner is not evidence.**
  ⚠ **TWO detectors, not one — the draft had only the first, and the second is the one that matters because `'[]'` is TERMINAL:** `subcategory_absent_drift` — **the field never arrived at all** (a broken prompt, schema or engine): window `swe_classified >= SUBCAT_DRIFT_MIN_SWE` AND window `swe_subcat_evaluated == 0`. `subcategory_empty_drift` — **the field arrived and every answer was `[]`** (a broken rubric): window `swe_subcat_evaluated >= SUBCAT_DRIFT_MIN_SWE` AND window `swe_subcategorized == 0`. ⚠ **Both are ABSOLUTE-zero tests, deliberately not ratios: a legitimate `[]` rate is high and layer-dependent, so a ratio would either miss real drift or flap, while "N SWE rows evaluated and not one carried a single slug" is unambiguous.**
  ⚠ **COUNTER SEMANTICS, stated once and used everywhere: `swe_subcat_evaluated` counts rows whose recorded subcategories value is NON-NULL, INCLUDING `[]` — this is COVERAGE and it is the definition the JVN coverage tile already uses. `swe_subcategorized` counts rows whose recorded list is NON-EMPTY — this is YIELD. They are different detectors and conflating them is what made the draft's single breaker blind.**
  ⚠ **ROLLING WINDOW, not a single tick. Measured on the live DB, SWE rows per tick over the last 20 ticks range 4-27 with a median of ~17, so a single-tick `N=10` threshold is SILENT on roughly a quarter of ticks.** `window_counters(store, n=SUBCAT_DRIFT_WINDOW_TICKS)` sums the three counters over the last `SUBCAT_DRIFT_WINDOW_TICKS = 3` ticks **INCLUDING the one being closed**; at the median that is ~51 SWE rows, **so the breaker is armed at essentially every tick-end.**
  `metrics.py`: add `SUBCAT_DRIFT_MIN_SWE = 10`, `SUBCAT_DRIFT_WINDOW_TICKS = 3`, the two predicates, and both flags in the payload beside `drift_suspected` (**:154**). Add all three counters to the fixed counters tuple (**:112-118**) — ⚠ **and they MUST also be in `_TICK_COUNTER_COLS` (handed to `ENR-ST-1` by `BF-1`) or `bump_tick_counters` drops them at store.py:632 with no error and both breakers read 0 forever while looking healthy.**
  ⚠ **`cli.py` `_cmd_tick_end` (:100-142): add a SECOND breaker block beside the existing `drift_suspected(tick)` check at :127-135, forcing `status='error'` and appending the reason to `notes`. Without this edit the flags ride in the payload but never trip the tick status — the existing mass-nulling breaker works precisely because it is wired here.**
  The Tier-1 classify path (`ENR-CLS-1`) bumps all three counters.
- *dependsOn:* `["BF-1","ENR-CLS-1"]`
- *tests:* ⚠ **THE CERTIFIED ASSERTION.**
  1. ⚠ **`test_all_empty_run_trips_the_breaker` — bump `swe_classified=12, swe_subcat_evaluated=12, swe_subcategorized=0` THROUGH `store.bump_tick_counters` (not by hand-writing the ticks row) across three ticks, run `cli tick-end`, and assert the PERSISTED tick row's `status == 'error'` and `'subcategory_empty_drift' in notes`. "Adds the flag" is not enough — the assertion is on the closed tick's status.**
  2. `test_one_labelled_row_closes_ok` — the same window with `swe_subcategorized=1` closes `status='ok'`. **The paired negative is what proves the breaker is not just always-on.**
  3. `test_absent_drift_at_the_boundary` — True at (classified=10, evaluated=0), **False at (9,0)**, False at (10,1).
  4. ⚠ **`test_window_spans_three_ticks` — 4/5/6 SWE rows across three ticks arms the breaker where a single tick would not (the coverage-gap regression).**
  5. ⚠ **`test_counters_are_not_silently_dropped` — all three counters appear in the payload AND reflect real increments through `bump_tick_counters`, asserted on the ticks ROW; this is what catches a column missing from `_TICK_COUNTER_COLS`.**
  6. The existing mass-nulling breaker still fires unchanged; tick-end names WHICH breaker in `notes` for each of the two conditions and leaves `status='ok'` when neither holds.
- *acceptance:* On a fixture DB the three-tick all-empty window closes `status='error'` with `subcategory_empty_drift` named in `notes`; `cli metrics-push --dry-run` shows both flags in the payload. · *risk:* low · *effort:* S — **two predicates, one window helper, one tick-end block; the difference between a silent failure and a loud one.**

**`DEP-3` — Post-deploy prod verification, including the cross-repo 15-slug parity check**

- *files:* `docs/RUNBOOK-subcategory-backfill.md`, `JVN/src/backend/api/tests/test_taxonomy_artifact.py`
- *changes:* Repointed from the deleted `OPS-5`; **the CI half is no longer invented here — `SCHEMA-15` owns it.** After JVN Phase 1: `alembic_version` moved; `GET /api/admin/enrichment/health` returns `sweOpenTotal` near the recorded denominator and ⚠ **`subcategoryUnknownSlugs = 0`**; `SELECT count(*) FROM job_categories` = **6**. After JVN Phase 2: `GET /api/jobs/facets` lists **15** subcategory slugs and **6** categories. After the enricher deploy: `PRAGMA user_version` = 4 read-only, `cli health` exits 0, `inbox.cleaned` trends down. ⚠ **THE PARITY CHECK IS THREE-WAY: prod facets == the enricher frozenset == the committed `taxonomy.json`, for both subcategories (15) and categories (6).** Runs `ENR-PAR-2`'s `cli parity-check` rather than an ad-hoc curl.

**`BF-9` — The run loop: separate lane, wall-clock deadline, two kill switches, three breakers**

- *files:* `backfill.py`, `cli.py`, `enrich_config.py`, `dashboard.py`, `tests/test_backfill.py`, `tests/test_enrich_config.py`
- *changes:* `cli subcategory-backfill [--until 09:00] [--tz America/Chicago] [--max-seconds N] [--limit N] [--batch N] [--model haiku|sonnet|opus] [--open-only] [--dry-run] [--probe-json PATH] [--notify]`.
  ⚠ **SEPARATION FROM THE LIVE LANE — four rules.** **(1) Own single-flight lock:** `lock.try_acquire(store.db_path.parent / '.backfill.lock', stale_after_hours=12)` (**signature verified at lock.py:93-95**); exit 75 if held, mirroring `tick-acquire`. ⚠ **It does NOT take `.tick.lock` — taking it would block the live worker for the whole night.** **(2) NEVER calls `store.start_tick()` or `store.bump_tick_counters()`.** ⚠ **Verified hazard: `bump_tick_counters` resolves `current_tick_id()` from meta (store.py:635) and the live worker restarts 62 s after each tick ends, so a tick is almost always open — every counter the backfill bumped would be silently attributed to the LIVE tick and corrupt both its metrics and the `drift_suspected` denominator, potentially forcing a healthy tick to close `status=error`.** Use `BF-1`'s ledger. **(3) NEVER calls `_write_queue_chunks` (cli.py:243)** and writes no chunk files — ⚠ **reusing the `classify-`/`judge-` prefixes would collide with a live tick's `_clear_chunks` glob (cli.py:234-240).** **(4) NOT added to `scripts/enrich_tick_wrapper.sh`** — it is launched by hand.
  ⚠ **CONCURRENCY IS 1 AND THERE IS NO CONCURRENCY KNOB.** `_call_with_repair` is strictly sequential (one `subprocess.run` per chunk, plus one on the repair path), nothing in `ChunkEngine` supports parallel chunks, and **a threadpool would multiply SQLite write contention against the live tick (`busy_timeout` is 5000 ms, store.py:196).** The draft's `--concurrency` flag and `backfill:concurrency` knob are **DELETED. Say so in the docstring so the option is not re-added on a slow night; the lever for wall time is `--batch` and the model tier, both measured by `P0-5`.**
  **LOOP, per chunk:** (a) re-read `backfill:enabled` from meta — the DB kill switch, honoured at chunk granularity; (b) stat `data/.backfill.stop` — the instant kill switch, no DB write needed; (c) check the wall-clock deadline; (d) `rows_needing_subcategory(limit=batch, max_attempts, open_only)` (`BF-4`); (e) ⚠ **run `EV-4`'s `subcategory_rules.label_row` pre-passes — rows they resolve are written immediately with `source='rule'` at ZERO model cost and never reach the model**; (f) `chunk_rows()` the remainder (`BF-6`) and call `engine.subcategory(rows)` (`ENR-PB-1`); (g) `match_results_to_chunk` (`BF-6`) — **echoed pair required, no positional stamping**; (h) validate each matched result through `tax.validate_subcategories`, dropping invalid slugs; ⚠ **if ALL slugs on a row were invalid, take the FAILURE disposition (attempts bump, stay NULL), NEVER `[]`**; (i) write via `record_subcategories(..., source='backfill', run_id=...)`; bump run counters.
  **DEADLINE:** `--until` is an absolute local wall-clock time resolved to its NEXT occurrence in `zoneinfo.ZoneInfo(--tz)` (**Python ≥ 3.11 per pyproject.toml:5, so DST-correct for CT**), combined with `--max-seconds` as `deadline = min(...)`. ⚠ **Checked BETWEEN chunks only, so a chunk in flight always completes its write — never killed mid-write.** On expiry: `end_backfill_run(status='ok', notes='deadline')`, exit 0.
  ⚠ **RESUMABILITY: nothing to implement. Unreached rows are still NULL with attempts unchanged, so the next run picks up exactly where this one stopped. No checkpoint file, no double-labelling.**
  ⚠ **ERROR TAXONOMY:** a TRANSPORT failure — `EngineError` from a non-zero `claude` exit, a subprocess timeout, or an `OSError` (claude_code.py:39-44) — **does NOT bump attempts, because a subscription rate-limit storm at 02:00 would otherwise retire thousands of good rows at the 3-attempt cap**; it increments `transport_errors` and, after `BACKFILL_TRANSPORT_ABORT = 5` **CONSECUTIVE** transport failures, aborts with `aborted_reason='transport'`. A PARSE failure (empty/degenerate array after `_call_with_repair` already retried once, an unanswered row, an echo mismatch, an all-invalid row) **DOES** bump attempts and increments `parse_errors`.
  ⚠ **THREE BREAKERS, all in-flight, all after `BACKFILL_BREAKER_MIN_N = 50` model-attempted rows.** **(1)** empty-array rate above `BACKFILL_BREAKER_EMPTY_RATIO` → abort `aborted_reason='empty_rate'`, exit non-zero. ⚠ **This exists because `'[]'` is TERMINAL: a systematically broken run does not error, it quietly writes "evaluated, no specialty applies" on thousands of rows and removes them from the queue permanently, recoverable only via `BF-14`.** **(2)** invalid-slug rate above 0.5 → `aborted_reason='invalid_slugs'` (a cross-repo taxonomy drift). **(3) NEW —** echo-mismatch rate above `BACKFILL_BREAKER_ECHO_RATIO` → `aborted_reason='echo_mismatch'`; ⚠ **a model that cannot echo ids reliably at batch B means B is too high, and the abort message says exactly that. This third breaker is what replaces positional repair operationally.**
  ⚠ **`--dry-run` runs the FULL pipeline and writes NOTHING** to `enrichments` or `backfill_runs`, emitting `data/backfill-probe-<ISO>.json` with per-chunk latency p50/p95/max, derived seconds/ROW at this batch, rule-pass yield split by title and tags, empty-array rate, invalid-slug rate, echo-mismatch rate and `model_calls`. ⚠ **`P0-5` measures with THIS harness rather than shipping a second one, so the probe and the real run cannot diverge.** Dry-run is also the permanent smoke test: **the safe way to exercise the whole path against prod data without writing a terminal `'[]'`.**
  **SIGTERM/SIGINT:** set the stop flag, finish the current chunk, close the run row cleanly.
  **KNOBS** in `enrich_config.py`, following `_get_bounded_int` (:109) / `_get_choice` (:97) / `get_judge_enabled` (:133): `backfill:enabled` (bool, ⚠ **default FALSE — ships dark**), `backfill:batch_size` (1..25, default 10), `backfill:max_attempts` (1..5, default 3). ⚠ **Every one must be added to `KNOB_META_KEYS` (:291-306) and `config_snapshot` (:270-285) or it never rides /metrics; to `build_parser`'s knob-setter table (cli.py:1347-1362); and to `dashboard._KNOB_SETTERS` (dashboard.py:53-66) or it cannot be toggled from the UI.**
  ⚠ **DOCUMENT (do not fix): `prompts._taxonomy_text` is `lru_cache(maxsize=1)` (prompts.py:24-26), so this — the first long-lived prompt-building process in the codebase — caches the rubric at first use and never sees a mid-run edit. That is the behaviour we want; the run row's `taxonomy_version` + `rubric_version` stamps prove which rubric produced the labels.**
- *dependsOn:* `["BF-1","BF-4","BF-6","BF-8","EV-4","ENR-TAX-2","ENR-PB-1"]`
- *tests:* A second concurrent invocation exits 75; ⚠ **the run NEVER calls `bump_tick_counters` (monkeypatch it to raise, assert the run completes) and never writes into `data/chunks/`**; a deadline already in the past stops before any model call and leaves every row NULL with attempts unchanged; `backfill:enabled` flipped off mid-run stops at the next chunk boundary; the `.backfill.stop` sentinel stops at the next chunk boundary; ⚠ **a fake engine returning `[]` for every row trips the empty-rate breaker after MIN_N and exits non-zero with `aborted_reason='empty_rate'`**; ⚠ **5 consecutive `EngineError`s abort with `aborted_reason='transport'` and NO row's attempts were bumped**; a parse failure DOES bump attempts; an echo-mismatch storm trips `aborted_reason='echo_mismatch'`; a hallucinated slug is dropped and the surviving slug recorded; ⚠ **an all-invalid row stays NULL**; `--dry-run` writes zero rows and zero ledger rows and emits every documented probe key; ⚠ **the CLI has no `--concurrency` flag (argparse assertion, so the deleted option cannot creep back)**. Config: defaults, bounds clamping, `KNOB_META_KEYS` set-equality against the getters, `config_snapshot` carries all three.
- *acceptance:* ⚠ **CANARY:** `cli subcategory-backfill --limit 200 --until <now+30m>` against the prod DB with `backfill:enabled on`. Afterwards `backfill_runs` has one `kind='label'` row with `status='ok'`, selected/rule_labelled/model_labelled/empty_labelled **summing to selected**, `SELECT COUNT(*) FROM enrichments WHERE sent_at IS NULL` **unchanged**, the live tick's counters **untouched**, and **30 rows hand-checked for label quality.** ⚠ **The canary's measured PER-LAYER empty-array rate is what sets `BACKFILL_BREAKER_EMPTY_RATIO` before any sustained run — the corpus-wide `[]` rate is 41.2%, but layer 3 is by construction the residue where the title says nothing and the tags disagree, so its true rate is far higher; a breaker tuned to 41% fires constantly.** · *risk:* **high** · *effort:* L — **the loop, the deadline, two kill switches, three breakers, the error taxonomy and the knob wiring; this is where a mistake is expensive.**

**`BF-14` — Scoped rollback: reset by run or by source, leaving schema, human work and JVN untouched**

- *files:* `store.py`, `cli.py`, `tests/test_store.py`, `tests/test_backfill.py`
- *changes:* ⚠ **Decision-record blocker #5, and the only thing that makes a systematically-wrong night recoverable — because `'[]'` is terminal in the tri-state, a bad run does not leave rows retryable.** `store.reset_subcategories(run_id=None, sources=None, category=None, limit=None, dry_run=False) -> int`:
  ```sql
  UPDATE enrichments SET subcategories = NULL, subcategory_confidence = NULL,
    subcategory_source = NULL, subcategory_run_id = NULL,
    subcategory_attempts = 0, subcategory_sent_at = NULL
  WHERE subcategory_source IN (...)  [AND subcategory_run_id = ?]  [AND category = ?]
  ```
  ⚠ **`subcategory_attempts = 0` is load-bearing and easy to omit: without it a row that hit the cap stays out of `rows_needing_subcategory` FOREVER and the reset silently fails to re-queue it. `subcategory_sent_at = NULL` is equally load-bearing now that `BF-12b` uses it as the cursor: leaving it set means the corrected label is never pushed.** ⚠ **NEVER touches rows whose `subcategory_source` is `'judge'` or `'human'`, and never touches `category`, `level`, `tags`, `locations`, any judge column, `sent_at`, `inbox.status`, or the schema.** Default `sources = ('rule','backfill')` — ⚠ **and the predicate accepts any of the FIVE values from §1.3, not a hardcoded 'backfill'; a value written by one repo and unrecognised by the reset's default tuple means the rollback silently misses rows.** `cli subcategory-reset [--run-id N] [--source rule|backfill|all] [--category C] [--dry-run] [--yes-i-mean-all]`: **prints the affected count FIRST** and refuses above `RESET_CONFIRM_THRESHOLD = 500` without `--yes-i-mean-all` — ⚠ **the same guard shape `ENR-REN-1` puts on `reenrich`, so the two dangerous commands in this repo behave identically under muscle memory.** **Run-scoping is why `BF-1` adds `subcategory_run_id`: if night 2 used a bad rubric, you reset night 2 and keep night 1's good rows.** ⚠ **STATED LIMIT, in the command's output and in the runbook: this resets the ENRICHER only. Rows already drained by `BF-12b` are live in JVN Postgres and this command cannot un-write them; the JVN-side statement is `ADM-15` and is a hard prerequisite for calling the rollback complete. Print the count of rows in scope whose `subcategory_sent_at` was non-NULL — that is exactly the set JVN still holds, so the operator knows the size of the second half of the job.**
- *dependsOn:* `["BF-1","BF-8"]`
- *tests:* Reset by `run_id` touches only that run's rows; `source='rule'` leaves backfill rows alone; ⚠ **a `subcategory_source='judge'` row and a human-corrected row both survive EVERY variant**; category/level/tags/judge columns and `sent_at`/`inbox.status` are byte-identical after a reset; ⚠ **`subcategory_attempts` really is 0 afterwards AND the row reappears in `rows_needing_subcategory` (the re-queue round trip)**; ⚠ **`subcategory_sent_at` is cleared so `BF-12b` will re-push the corrected label**; label 10 rows in a run, reset that run, re-run the backfill and the same 10 rows are selected again; the command reports the count of already-drained rows separately.
- *acceptance:* On a copy of prod after the canary: `cli subcategory-reset --run-id 1 --dry-run` prints exactly the canary's row count, **names how many of those were already pushed to JVN, and mutates nothing**; the real run zeroes them and `subcategory_backlog_counts()['eligible']` returns to its pre-canary value. · *risk:* low · *effort:* S — **one UPDATE plus the confirmation guard; it is the safety net for the whole workstream.**

**`BF-16` — Measure the per-request write-back cost and derive the drain pace (D2's phase 0)**

- *files:* `backfill.py`, `cli.py`, `tests/test_backfill.py`, `docs/BACKFILL.md`
- *changes:* ⚠ **RE-SCOPED to the `SCHEMA-14` endpoint (the D2 ruling): the draft's two-payload-shape comparison (`full` vs `nolocations` through `/results`) is DELETED, because the slim contract has exactly one shape.** ⚠ **The rate at which the drain may push MUST be derived from a measured per-request cost, not invented — the draft's "200 per tick" was a guess and it is the number that produced the two-week lag.** `cli subcategory-push-probe --batches N [--sizes 50,100,250,500]` measures, **against PROD JVN from the prod checkout**, using rows JVN already holds (so each POST is an idempotent re-write): **`T_req(B)` p50/p95/max** — wall-clock of one `POST /api/internal/enrichment/subcategories` carrying B items; **`T_ping` p50** — wall-clock of a trivial `GET /api/internal/enrichment/health` (⚠ **verified to exist at internal_enrichment.py:330; reached through the same `_url` helper at jvn_client.py:48-49 so it carries the same internal-key header**), the network-RTT baseline; ⚠ **`S(B) = T_req_p95 − T_ping_p50` — the estimated POOL-SLOT HOLD TIME. This is the number that matters, not the request count: the endpoint takes ONE pooled connection via `Depends(get_db)` for the WHOLE batch and commits once at the end, so slot occupancy scales with batch size, not with request count.** Plus `bytes(B)` to bound egress.
  **DERIVE and print, in the probe output and in the runbook table:** `sleep_between_batches = S_p95 * (100 / duty_pct - 1)`; `rows_per_hour = 3600 * B * (duty_pct / 100) / S_p95`; `eta_hours = pending_push / rows_per_hour`. ⚠ **At concurrency 1 the drain occupies at most ONE of the pool's 15 slots at any instant and, time-averaged, `duty_pct/100` of one slot. The 2026-05-17 incident (docs/incidents/2026-05-17-recent-jobs-pool-exhaustion.md) established the violated invariant as "N parallel DB-backed requests must stay <= the pool slot count" — verified in code at `init_pool(dsn, minconn=1, maxconn=15, timeout=5.0)` (dependencies.py:31) with `semaphore.acquire(timeout=_pool_timeout)` raising `RuntimeError('Timed out waiting for a database connection')` at dependencies.py:88-89. N=1 satisfies it structurally; the duty cap bounds time-averaged occupancy on top.** *(Correction: the draft said `semaphore.acquire(timeout=5.0)` — 5.0 is not a literal in that line; it is `_pool_timeout`, defaulted from `init_pool`.)*
  ⚠ **CHOOSE from the measurement, and record the reasoning in the runbook: the push batch is the LARGEST B whose `S_p95` stays under `PUSH_SLOT_HOLD_CEILING_S` (placeholder 5.0 s, i.e. comfortably inside the 5 s semaphore timeout so the drain can never be the request that pushes another request past it) — batch size is capped by SLOT-HOLD TIME, NOT by request count, which is the opposite of the usual instinct.** Hard ceiling **500** (`SCHEMA-14` 422s above it). ⚠ **Also set `PUSH_HTTP_TIMEOUT_S = max(30.0, 3 * T_req_p95)` and pass it to `JVNClient(timeout=...)`: at the default `http_timeout_seconds = 30.0` (config.py:23) a slow batch times out CLIENT-side while the server keeps holding its slot, and an immediate retry then stacks a second slot — the exact way a throttle turns into the incident.**
- *dependsOn:* `["SCHEMA-14","BF-8"]`
- *tests:* (respx) the probe issues exactly `batches * len(sizes)` POSTs plus the pings, ⚠ **never concurrently (assert monotonic non-overlap of request timestamps)**; the derived `sleep_between_batches` and `rows_per_hour` match the formula on hand-supplied timings; ⚠ **a probe run writes no `enrichments` changes locally — `subcategory_sent_at` is untouched (it is a measurement, not a push).**
- *acceptance:* `data/subcategory-push-probe-<ISO>.json` exists with non-null p50/p95 for at least 3 of the 4 cells; the runbook records the chosen (batch, duty_pct) pair **with the numbers that justified it and the derived rows/hour and ETA.** Afterwards, against prod Postgres: ⚠ **`SELECT count(*) FROM job_tags` and `SELECT count(*) FROM job_locations` are unchanged from the pre-probe snapshot.** · *risk:* medium · *effort:* M — **the probe is small; the value is that it converts every rate in `BF-12b` from a guess into a measurement, and it touches prod Postgres.**

**`P0-5` — Measure real seconds/row for the subcategory-only prompt — haiku vs sonnet, on the same sample**

- *files:* `scripts/measure_subcategory_cost.py`, `docs/RUNBOOK-subcategory-backfill.md`, `tests/test_measure_subcategory_cost.py`
- *changes:* ⚠ **MOVED TO PR-E and its edge REVERSED (§0 ruling 10): it consumes `BF-9`'s `--dry-run` harness rather than shipping a second probe, so the edge runs `BF-9 → P0-5`, not the reverse. Contract decision 18's `BF-9.dependsOn += P0-5` would have been a CYCLE.** Absorbs deleted `BF-7`. 20 rows sampled uniformly from `P0-4`'s scope; ⚠ **each prompt built from TITLE + `enrichments.tags` ONLY, never `clean_description` — the injection boundary from `claude_code.py`'s docstring (:7-10), enforceable because 10,792 of the 11,150 local SWE rows carry non-empty tags.** ⚠ **STATE THE DISCLOSURE the verifiers asked for: those are the ENRICHER's own v6-generated tags, not company tags (JVN's `_JOB_PROJECTION` at internal_enrichment.py:61-72 has never shipped `details->'tags'`), so layer-2 tag agreement is NOT independent evidence — say it in the runbook and the morning report.** **DECISION RULE: lowest p95 s/row with parse-failure ≤ 2% and haiku-vs-sonnet agreement ≥ 85%; below 85%, sonnet wins.** ⚠ **ALSO folds in `BF-7`'s per-layer `[]`-rate measurement — `BF-9`'s empty-rate breaker threshold must be that MEASURED number plus a stated band, not a placeholder.** `ChunkEngine._call_with_repair` is strictly sequential, so **write the concurrency arithmetic down rather than assuming it.**

- *tests:* The sampler never selects a row outside `backfill_scope`; ⚠ **the prompt renderer NEVER includes `clean_description` — assert the substring is absent from every rendered prompt (the injection-boundary regression).**
- *acceptance:* The runbook's cost table has real p50/p95 for all six tier×chunk cells **with the chosen cell circled**, and the projected whole-run duration computed as `matched_local * <chosen s/row>`. · *risk:* medium · *effort:* M

**`BF-12b` — Throttled bulk push: drain backfilled labels to JVN in hours, on `SCHEMA-14`'s partial contract**

- *files:* `store.py`, `backfill.py`, `cli.py`, `jvn_client.py`, `enrich_config.py`, `dashboard.py`, `tests/test_backfill.py`, `tests/test_store.py`
- *changes:* ⚠ **THE D2 RULING, APPLIED (§0 ruling 3).** The draft's `subcategory-reopen` drain is DELETED entirely — the `record_subcategories(reopen=True)` variant, the `writeback:subcategory_reopen_per_run` knob, and the `scripts/enrich_tick_wrapper.sh` edit. **Three verified reasons:** (a) clearing `sent_at` puts the drained rows into `enriched_unsent()`, which takes `limit=None` (**store.py:542-559**), **so the next tick's `write_back` ships an unbounded burst the drain cannot pace**; (b) riding the tick caps the drain at one slice per tick, and at ~12.8 ticks/day **that is the two-week lag the verifier found**; (c) re-opening flips `inbox.status` back to `'enriched'`, **so a drained row is temporarily indistinguishable from a genuinely unshipped one and the poison-row `attempts` machinery starts counting drain failures against real rows.** ⚠ **AND the draft's replacement — re-POSTing the full `/results` payload minus the two locations keys — is ALSO deleted, in favour of `SCHEMA-14`'s endpoint. One sentence for the record: `apply_result` returns early for any row with `human_corrected_at` set (verified enrichment_writer.py:102-113) while still counting it `written`, so the `/results` route can NEVER write a backfilled label onto the human-corrected pool the eval loop depends on, and it re-ships every row's full `clean_description` to set two columns; `SCHEMA-14` skips only on `enrichment_subcategory_source='human'` and holds one pooled connection for two narrow UPDATEs.**
  **THE REPLACEMENT — a separate, paced, resumable process that never touches `sent_at` or `inbox.status`:**
  `store.rows_needing_subcategory_push(limit)` — `SELECT e.*, i.clean_description FROM enrichments e JOIN inbox i ON … WHERE e.subcategories IS NOT NULL AND e.subcategory_sent_at IS NULL AND e.sent_at IS NOT NULL AND i.status != 'failed' ORDER BY e.enriched_at LIMIT ?`. The same JOIN and the same `status != 'failed'` exclusion as `enriched_unsent` (**store.py:547-552**). ⚠ **The `sent_at IS NOT NULL` clause makes the two lanes DISJOINT BY CONSTRUCTION: `sent_at IS NULL` belongs to the live write-back, `sent_at IS NOT NULL AND subcategory_sent_at IS NULL` belongs to the drain. Neither can ship the other's rows and there is no lock between them.**
  `store.mark_subcategory_sent(pairs)` — ⚠ **stamps `subcategory_sent_at` ONLY. Never writes `sent_at`, never writes `inbox.status`.**
  New `JVNClient.post_subcategories(items)` beside `post_results` (**jvn_client.py:124-138**), same `_url` helper and internal-key header.
  `backfill.push_subcategories(store, client=None, ...)` — the paced loop. Per batch: build the **slim** items (`source_id`, `job_listing_id`, `subcategories`, `subcategory_confidence`, `subcategory_source`, `taxonomy_version` — ⚠ **and nothing else, because the endpoint is `extra='forbid'`**); POST via `JVNClient(timeout=PUSH_HTTP_TIMEOUT_S)`; on a 2xx ⚠ **reuse `writeback._match_failed_pairs` (writeback.py:83-125) VERBATIM so the ambiguous-bare-id semantics are identical rather than re-derived**; stamp the success pairs; then sleep.
  **RATE, all derived from `BF-16`'s measurement — nothing invented:** `writeback:subcategory_push_batch` (bounds 1..500, default 250); `writeback:subcategory_push_duty_pct` (bounds 1..50, default 25); `writeback:subcategory_push_max_seconds` (bounds 0..21600, default 3600 — ⚠ **so an unattended run cannot drift into peak hours**); `writeback:send_subcategories` (`ENR-WB-1`'s knob) — ⚠ **the command REFUSES with exit 2 when it is OFF.** ⚠ **CONCURRENCY IS HARD-CODED 1 AND THERE IS NO KNOB. One in-flight request = one of the pool's 15 slots. A comment above the loop names docs/incidents/2026-05-17-recent-jobs-pool-exhaustion.md and states that the invariant it established — N parallel DB-backed requests ≤ pool slots — is satisfied structurally here and MUST NOT be traded for speed.** ⚠ **The command PRINTS, before the first POST: batch, duty, derived sleep, derived rows/hour, `pending_push`, and the derived ETA in hours. If the printed ETA is unacceptable the lever is `duty_pct`, and raising it is a deliberate, logged act. This ETA is what replaces "overnight" as the plan's stated claim, and it must be written into the runbook next to it.**
  **FAILURE / RETRY:** reuse `writeback.TRANSIENT_HTTP_STATUSES` (**verified writeback.py:32, `{429,500,502,503,504}`**) — **imported, not redefined.** Per batch: up to 3 attempts with `2 ** attempt` backoff, mirroring `write_back`'s loop (**:180-215**). ⚠ **ADAPTIVE BACKOFF: any transient status also DOUBLES `sleep_between_batches` for the rest of the run (capped at 60 s), decaying 20% back toward the derived value after each clean batch. A per-batch retry alone keeps arriving at the same rate, which is precisely wrong when the far side is under pressure.** ⚠ **POOL-PRESSURE DETECTOR: a 5xx whose body contains `Timed out waiting for a database connection` — the exact string raised at dependencies.py:89 — is recorded as a pool-pressure event, immediately doubles the sleep, and after `PUSH_POOL_ABORT = 3` events in one run ABORTS with `aborted_reason='pool_pressure'` and a non-zero exit. This is the direct, named honouring of the incident.** ⚠ **CLIENT-SIDE TIMEOUT: on `httpx.TimeoutException` the drain waits `PUSH_TIMEOUT_COOLDOWN = 30 s` before the next attempt — the server may still be committing and holding its slot; an immediate retry stacks a second one.** Non-transient 4xx (including 413/422) **aborts immediately — it means a knob is mis-set and retrying cannot help.** Per-row `failed[]` entries do NOT get `subcategory_sent_at` (they retry on the next run) and increment `push_rejected`. ⚠ **A `skipped[]` entry naming the human lock DOES get `subcategory_sent_at` (terminal) and increments `push_skipped_human`.** A warning naming a nulled subcategory increments `push_nulled_by_jvn` — ⚠ **non-zero means the cross-repo 15-slug parity is broken and the report says so.**
  **OPERATIONAL SHAPE:** `cli subcategory-push [--limit N] [--batch N] [--duty N] [--max-seconds N] [--dry-run] [--notify]`. Own single-flight lock `data/.subcategory-push.lock` (`stale_after_hours=6`), exit 75 if held. Kill switch `data/.subcategory-push.stop` checked between batches; SIGTERM/SIGINT finishes the batch in flight and closes the run row. Opens a `kind='push'` row in `backfill_runs` **so the morning report reads one ledger.** ⚠ **RESUMABILITY: `subcategory_sent_at` IS the cursor — no offset, no checkpoint, re-running after any interruption resumes exactly.** All four knobs go into `KNOB_META_KEYS` (**:291-306**), `config_snapshot` (**:270-285**), the CLI knob-setter table (**cli.py:1347-1362**) and `dashboard._KNOB_SETTERS` (**:53-66**).
  **HOW THE JVN-SIDE COVERAGE COUNTER TRACKS IT:** `ADM-6`'s tile is computed in Postgres and rises row-by-row as the drain lands. ⚠ **The enricher's reconciliation identity, printed by `BF-10`, is `pushed − push_skipped_human − push_nulled_by_jvn == JVN's swe_subcategorized delta`, with `pending_push` as the entire remaining gap. Any difference LARGER than `pending_push` is a bug on one side and the report names which side is behind, so the lag is never read as breakage.**
- *dependsOn:* `["BF-8","BF-9","BF-16","SCHEMA-14","ENR-WB-1"]`
- *tests:* `rows_needing_subcategory_push` returns only rows matching all four predicates, excludes `i.status='failed'`, and respects `limit`; **a row already carrying `subcategory_sent_at` is never re-selected (termination)**; ⚠ **DISJOINTNESS: for a fixture covering every combination, no row is returned by BOTH `enriched_unsent()` and `rows_needing_subcategory_push()`**; `mark_subcategory_sent` leaves `sent_at` and `inbox.status` byte-identical. (respx) ⚠ **the command refuses with exit 2 when `writeback:send_subcategories` is off**; ⚠ **the payload's key set is EXACTLY the six slim keys (an extra key would 422 against `extra='forbid'`)**; requests are strictly sequential and the observed gap is within tolerance of the derived sleep; a 429 doubles the sleep and it decays back after clean batches; ⚠ **three responses carrying the `'Timed out waiting for a database connection'` string abort with `aborted_reason='pool_pressure'` and a non-zero exit**; a 413 aborts immediately without retrying; an `httpx.TimeoutException` waits the cooldown before retrying; a `failed[]` entry leaves that row unstamped and increments `push_rejected`; ⚠ **a human-lock `skipped[]` entry DOES stamp the row and increments `push_skipped_human`**; a second invocation exits 75; the `.subcategory-push.stop` sentinel stops at the next batch boundary; ⚠ **there is no `--concurrency` flag and no concurrency knob (argparse + `KNOB_META_KEYS` assertions).**
- *acceptance:* With the knob off, `cli subcategory-push` exits 2 and posts nothing. With it on, against prod after the `BF-9` canary: `cli subcategory-push --limit 200` moves exactly 200 rows, prints an ETA computed from `BF-16`'s numbers, and afterwards ⚠ **`SELECT COUNT(*) FROM enrichments WHERE sent_at IS NULL` is UNCHANGED and `SELECT COUNT(*) FROM inbox WHERE status='enriched'` is UNCHANGED (the two invariants that separate this from the deleted re-open drain).** On the JVN side, `SELECT count(*) FROM job_listings WHERE enrichment_subcategories IS NOT NULL` rises by exactly 200 minus the reported `push_skipped_human`, ⚠ **and `job_tags`, `job_locations` and `job_enrichment.clean_description` are all unchanged.** A second invocation before any further labelling moves 200 DIFFERENT rows. **Full-corpus drain completes within the ETA the command printed, measured and recorded in the runbook.** · *risk:* **high** · *effort:* L — **small code, but it is the only step in this workstream that generates sustained load against prod Postgres, and the incident it must not repeat is documented.**

**`BF-10` — Morning report: one ledger, both lanes, and the JVN-coverage reconciliation**

- *files:* `backfill.py`, `cli.py`, `health.py`, `tests/test_backfill.py`
- *changes:* `cli subcategory-report [--run-id N] [--kind label|push] [--json] [--notify]`, defaulting to the latest run of each kind. Prints, **and writes `data/backfill-report-<run_id>.json` so 09:00 has an artifact even if the terminal is gone.** **LABEL LANE:** rows labelled split by source (rule / model / empty); rows remaining eligible; ⚠ **rows RETIRED at the attempts cap (the honest number the discarded `SEL-1` had nowhere to show)**; errors split transport vs parse; ⚠ **`echo_mismatch` and `unanswered` called out SEPARATELY from parse errors, because a non-zero echo-mismatch count means the batch size is too high and the fix is a knob, not a rerun — this is the counter that replaced positional repair and it must be visible or the replacement is inert**; `model_calls`, wall time, **derived seconds/row for THIS run (so the next night's length is predicted from last night rather than from a 20-row probe)**; ⚠ **`status` and `aborted_reason` loudly, at the top, when the run aborted.** **PUSH LANE:** `pushed`, `push_batches`, `push_rejected`, `push_skipped_human`, `push_nulled_by_jvn`, `pool_pressure_events`, ⚠ **the effective sleep at the end of the run (so an adaptive backoff that never decayed is visible)**, and `pending_push` with the derived ETA from `BF-16`'s formula. ⚠ **RECONCILIATION BLOCK — the number Decision 3's 90% flip is read off, from BOTH sides:** `local_coverage = (labelled + empty) / total_swe` (the enricher's view) and `shipped_coverage = (rows with subcategory_sent_at) / total_swe` (what JVN should be able to see). **The identity is `shipped − push_skipped_human − push_nulled_by_jvn == JVN's swe_subcategorized delta`, and `pending_push` is the ENTIRE legitimate gap. Print all of it with one line naming which side is behind, so an operator comparing the enricher report with the JVN admin tile reads a drain in progress rather than a bug.** `push_nulled_by_jvn > 0` prints a WARNING naming the parity check. ⚠ **`health.collect_health` (verified health.py:18) gains a `subcategory` key** — `{eligible, labelled, empty, stuck, total_swe, local_coverage_pct, shipped_coverage_pct, pending_push, last_label_run, last_push_run}` — plus **`starved_null_recency`** (absorbed from deleted `OPS-1`; the number `P0-3` deliberately does not repair). ⚠ **Verified: `collect_health` is the ONE function feeding both `cli health` and the dashboard's `/api/health`, so a single edit surfaces both.** `--notify` sends a one-line summary via `~/.claude/skills/message-brendan/send.sh` — **opt-in only, never the default, and a send failure must not change the exit code.**

- *changes:* `savedFiltersApi.ts` is unchanged by #252; anchors verified. ⚠ **Every stored saved-filters row predates this feature and has no `subcategory` key.** `validateSavedFilters` (**:91-121**) throws on any field that is not a string array (category **:104-106**, level **:107-109**) and ends with an UNMODIFIED `return res as unknown as SavedFilters;` at **:120**. ⚠ **TWO failure modes if handled naively: a strict `!Array.isArray(res.subcategory)` check mirroring :104-106 throws for EVERY existing user and takes down ALL saved filters — time windows, locations, keyword lists — not just the new field; and a merely lenient check that returns `res` unchanged leaves `subcategory` undefined, so `draftFromServer`'s `[...p.subcategory]` (SavedFiltersPage.tsx:46-56) throws a TypeError on the Saved Filters page.** **SO DO BOTH:** **(a)** a TOLERANT check inserted after the level check at :109 — `if (res.subcategory !== undefined && (!Array.isArray(res.subcategory) || res.subcategory.some((s) => typeof s !== 'string'))) throw new Error('Invalid /api/users/saved-filters response: subcategory must be a string array');`; **(b)** ⚠ **NORMALIZE on the way out — replace :120 with a spread supplying `subcategory: Array.isArray(res.subcategory) ? res.subcategory : []`.**
- *dependsOn:* `["FE-CT-1"]` · *tests:* NEW `__tests__/features/savedFilters/validateSavedFilters.test.ts`: a legacy payload with no `subcategory` key validates and yields `subcategory: []`; `['backend']` passes through; `'backend'` throws; `[1]` throws; ⚠ **the existing strict checks on category/level/locations still throw exactly as before (regression guard — the whole risk here is widening one check and accidentally loosening its neighbours).** · *acceptance:* `npm test -- --run savedFilters` passes, and ⚠ **`npm run type-check` fails at EXACTLY the five fixture sites `FE-TS-3` then fixes (propagateSavedFilters.test.ts lines 49/83/120/147 and useHydrateSavedFilters.test.tsx line 65) and nowhere else.** · *risk:* medium · *effort:* S

**`RA-5` — PHASE 2 — search router: the `?subcategory=` param, validation, and the cursor fingerprint**

- *files:* `routers/jobs_search.py`, `api/tests/test_jobs_search_contract.py`
- *changes:* **BRANCH-ONLY.** Anchors: `_CATEGORY_RE` **:139**, `_LEVEL_RE` :140; `_validate_slugs` **:162** with `_MAX_FACET_VALUES = 20` at **:73** enforced :174-177; the category/level `Query(...)` declarations **:282-292**; `compute_filter_fingerprint` called at **:400** over the 8-key dict :401-410; the `filters: SearchFilters` literal **:439-449**; ⚠ **`StaleCursorError` → 409 at :417-426 (the draft said 401-411)**; ⚠ **`pagination.compute_filter_fingerprint` is at `src/backend/api/pagination.py:301` — NOT `services/pagination.py`, and it exists only on #252**; `_SEARCH_CURSOR_VERSION='s1'` :286. **(1)** ⚠ **`_SUBCATEGORY_RE = re.compile(r"\A[a-z_]{1,40}\Z")` — `\A`/`\Z`, NEVER `^`/`$`: Python's `$` also matches before a trailing newline, so `?subcategory=backend%0A` would validate then match no row — a silent 200 where the contract promises 422. The hazard is documented in this file at :121-127.** **(2)** Repeatable `subcategory: list[str] | None = Query(default=None, …)` beside `level`, its description stating the OR/AND semantics, the implied SWE parent, the full_stack widening and the NULL/empty hiding. **(3)** `_validate_slugs(subcategory, pattern=_SUBCATEGORY_RE, field='subcategory')` beside :365-366 — free cap/422/dedupe, ⚠ **and a well-formed UNKNOWN slug deliberately NOT an error.** **(4)** `"subcategories": subcategories` in the `filters` literal (**totality makes omission a type error**). **(5)** ⚠ **THE FINGERPRINT: build the dict as a local and add the key ONLY when the filter is active — an absent key keeps every existing cursor valid.**
- *dependsOn:* `["RA-4"]` · *tests:* two values parse; `?subcategory=Backend` → 422; `?subcategory=` → 422; ⚠ **`?subcategory=backend%0A` → 422 (the `\Z` regression)**; 21 values → 400; duplicates dedupe order-preservingly. · *acceptance:* page a two-page subcategory walk, confirm page 2's cursor is accepted, then replay it with `subcategory` changed and confirm a **409**. · *risk:* medium · *effort:* S · *param:* ⚠ **the literal pre-change cursor string the no-churn test pins must be captured by RUNNING the current build against a fixed filter set — a wrong literal makes the test assert the opposite of what it is for.**

**`RA-8` — Verify the prod query plan and settle the index question (measure, do not guess)**

- *files:* `services/job_search.py`, `db_models.py`
- *changes:* Repointed `['RA-4','MIG-1','ENR-BACKFILL-CANARY'] → ['RA-4','SCHEMA-1','BF-9']`. ⚠ **AFTER the backfill canary has produced a realistic distribution — NOT before, because an all-NULL column makes every plan look free** — run `EXPLAIN (ANALYZE, BUFFERS)` on prod for **a common bucket (`backend`), the largest (`infrastructure_platform`) and the rarest (`quantitative`)**, on the real page query, **once for page 1 and once with a deep cursor.** ⚠ **Two plans compete and which wins is unknowable until the data exists: an ordered seek on `idx_job_listings_open_category_keyset` with `&&` as a heap filter, or a GIN bitmap plus a Sort. HARD RULE either way: a subcategory must NEVER be wedged as a fifth equality column into the keyset index.** ⚠ **THRESHOLD IS PRE-COMMITTED: >250 ms or >20,000 buffers on either query ships `SCHEMA-12`; below both, do not ship it and record the numbers.**
- *dependsOn:* `["RA-4","SCHEMA-1","BF-9"]` · *tests:* none — a measurement. Add a captured-SQL assertion only if the predicate shape changes as a result. · *acceptance:* six EXPLAINs (page 1 and a page-20 cursor, three bucket sizes) **each under 150 ms, pasted into the PR with a one-line statement of which index the planner chose.** · *risk:* low · *effort:* S

**`FE-CH-1` — Job card chips: the subcategory chip SUBSTITUTES for the category chip, with the empty case spelled out**

- *files:* `components/shared/JobCard/JobChipsSection.tsx`, `components/shared/JobCard/JobListingCard.tsx`
- *changes:* Record Decision 8 item 1: ⚠ **substitute, do not add.**
  ⚠ **ANCHOR NOTE — READ BEFORE EDITING.** The department chip is **already gone on `main`**: PR #260 "Remove the ATS department chip from the job card" (`d6433b3`) merged at **06:35 on 2026-08-20**, and this branch carries the equivalent commit `7a30679`. ⚠ **Main's version KEEPS `Job.department` and the company-page department filter — only the CHIP was removed — so do not "clean up" the model field.** ⚠ **Re-cut the anchors against MAIN's post-#260 `JobChipsSection.tsx`, where the explanatory comment now sits around lines 28-31.** On that file: the props interface is **:4-13** with `level` last at **:12**; `facetLabel` is declared at **:16** with the `slug.split('_').join(' ')` fallback at **:17**; the chip row is **:39-43** with the category chip at **:41**. ⚠ **`:21` is #252's PRE-merge number — #252's copy still carries `department?: string` at :8 and has not taken #260. Re-cut after the merge; the post-merge number is :17.** `JobListingCard.tsx:144` is the only mount in the app (verified by grep), **and #252 touches neither file, so there is no conflict.**
  Add `subcategories?: string[] | null;` after `level` (**:12**) and replace **:41** with a conditional that has an **EXPLICIT empty branch** — ⚠ **this closes verify-0's "chip fallback for `[]` is unspecified" gap, which matters because ~9% of SWE rows end at `[]` permanently and 100% of them do for the whole backfill window; a literal "the subcategory chip REPLACES the category chip" substitution would render NO category chip at all for those rows:**
  ```tsx
  {revealSubcategories && subcategories?.length
    ? subcategories.map((s) => (
        <Tooltip key={s} title={`${facetLabel(category ?? 'software_engineering')} › ${facetLabel(s)}`}>
          <Chip label={facetLabel(s)} size="small" variant="filled" />
        </Tooltip>
      ))
    : category && <Chip label={facetLabel(category)} size="small" variant="filled" />}
  ```
  So: **labelled → the specialty chips (index 0 first); `[]` → the CATEGORY chip; `null` → the CATEGORY chip; flag off → the CATEGORY chip.** ⚠ **`facetLabel`'s fallback at :17 must never be reached for a known slug, which is what `FE-CT-2`'s `FACET_LABELS` fold guarantees.**
- *dependsOn:* `["FE-CT-1","FE-CT-2","FE-FG-1"]`
- *tests:* Extend `__tests__/components/shared/JobCard/JobChipsSection.test.tsx` (**existing cases at :33, :41, :47; the "exactly three chips" assertion is at :53, not :52**). With the reveal context stubbed **TRUE**: `subcategories: ['backend']` renders 'Backend' and ⚠ **does NOT render 'Software Engineering'**; two subcategories render both **in the given order (primary first)**; ⚠ **`subcategories: []` renders the CATEGORY chip (the substitution's empty branch); `subcategories: null` likewise**; an unknown slug falls back to spaced words. With the context **FALSE**: ⚠ **`subcategories: ['backend']` still renders 'Software Engineering' — the flag-off path is byte-identical to today.** In every case Remote and level are unaffected, ⚠ **and the "exactly three chips" count at :53 still holds for a single-labelled job (that IS the substitution, proven).**
- *acceptance:* `npm test -- --run JobChipsSection` passes; in the app a backfilled SWE card shows 'Backend' with a 'Software Engineering › Backend' tooltip and **no duplicate category chip**, while an un-backfilled SWE card is unchanged. · *risk:* low · *effort:* S
