## 4. Phase-0 parameters — every value deliberately left unmeasured, and what sets it

| Parameter | Placeholder | What sets it |
|---|---|---|
| `PARAM_BACKFILL_DENOMINATOR` (`matched_local`) | ~8,126 | **`P0-4`'s importer output.** ⚠ **8,126 is a JVN-side OPEN-SWE count, NOT a backfillable count.** It replaces the placeholder in every downstream estimate **and in the 90% coverage denominator**; `ADM-6`'s `swe_open_total` must use the same definition or the tile and the backfill disagree about what 90% means. |
| `SECONDS_PER_ROW_P50` / `P95`, `MODEL_TIER` (haiku\|sonnet), `CHUNK_SIZE`, `CLAUDE_TIMEOUT_SECONDS` | — | **`P0-5`'s 20-row sweep**, run through `BF-9`'s `--dry-run` harness. ⚠ **The only measured figure on this machine is 137.9 s/item for the FULL classify prompt on qwen3:32b — a different model, prompt and engine. Do not reuse the plan's 60 s/row or 20 s/row placeholders, and repeat no night-length claim until P0-5's table exists.** |
| `BACKFILL_BREAKER_EMPTY_RATIO` | 0.60 | **`BF-9`'s 200-row canary, measured PER LAYER.** ⚠ **The corpus-wide `[]` rate is 41.2%, but layer 3 is by construction the residue where the title says nothing and the tags disagree, so its true rate is far above the corpus figure. A breaker tuned to the corpus number fires constantly; one tuned loose misses real breakage.** |
| `BACKFILL_BREAKER_ECHO_RATIO` | 0.20 | **`P0-5`'s batch sweep.** ⚠ **The batch at which the echo-mismatch rate starts rising is the ceiling on `backfill:batch_size`, and this breaker is what makes that ceiling enforce itself at 02:00.** |
| `SUBCAT_DRIFT_WINDOW_TICKS` | 3 | Justified by the measured **4-27 SWE rows per tick (median ~17)** over the last 20 ticks. ⚠ **Re-measure before shipping; if the median moves the window moves with it, because the whole point is that the breaker is armed on essentially every tick-end rather than a quarter of them.** |
| `SUBCAT_DRIFT_MIN_SWE` | 10 | Same measurement; at the median window (~51 rows) this arms on nearly every tick-end. |
| `T_req(B)` p50/p95, `T_ping` p50, `S_p95 = T_req_p95 − T_ping_p50`, `bytes(B)` | — | **`BF-16`**, against prod from the prod checkout, at B ∈ {50, 100, 250, 500}. ⚠ **`S_p95` is the estimated POOL-SLOT HOLD TIME and is the ONLY input the drain rate is allowed to be derived from. No rows/second appears anywhere in this plan as a constant.** |
| `writeback:subcategory_push_batch` | 250 (bounds 1..500) | ⚠ **The LARGEST B whose measured `S_p95` stays under `PUSH_SLOT_HOLD_CEILING_S` (5.0 s — inside the pool semaphore's 5 s acquire timeout at dependencies.py:88, so the drain can never be the request that pushes another past it). Batch size is capped by SLOT-HOLD TIME, not by request count.** Hard ceiling 500 (`SCHEMA-14` 422s above it). |
| `writeback:subcategory_push_duty_pct` | 25 (bounds 1..50) | Chosen against the acceptable ETA. `sleep = S_p95 * (100/duty − 1)`; `rows_per_hour = 3600·B·(duty/100)/S_p95`. ⚠ **Both are computed and PRINTED at runtime, never hardcoded, and the resulting ETA is what replaces the draft's invented "200 per tick".** |
| `PUSH_SLOT_HOLD_CEILING_S` | 5.0 | Chosen to sit inside the pool semaphore's acquire timeout; confirm against `BF-16`'s distribution. |
| `PUSH_HTTP_TIMEOUT_S` | `max(30.0, 3 × T_req_p95)` | **`BF-16`.** ⚠ **The default `http_timeout_seconds` of 30.0 (config.py:23) can expire client-side while the server still holds its slot, and an immediate retry then stacks a second one — the exact way a throttle turns into the incident.** |
| `MIGRATION_V3_V4_SECONDS` | — | **`P0-7`**, on a `sqlite3 ".backup"` copy of the 240 MB prod DB. ⚠ **DECISION RULE: >30 s means splitting the migration, because `deploy.sh` runs it at step 6 with the worker parked.** |
| `_CLASSIFY_PROMPT_MAX_CHARS` | ~33,719 | **`ENR-LAT-1`** measures `len(build_classify_prompt(fixture))` on the committed v7 files; the constant is set to that value **rounded up to the next 500**. ⚠ **Do NOT hardcode the estimate.** |
| `CLASSIFY_LATENCY_CEILING_PCT` | proposed 10% | **`ENR-LAT-1`.** ⚠ **Cannot be invented: it is a function of the v6 p50/p95 baseline `ENR-LAT-1` itself measures against a PAUSED worker, and of the lane's measured 485/day against 495-552 arriving. It must state the number AND the arithmetic (classifications/day lost per 1% latency).** |
| `PARAM_CLASSIFY_PROMPT_BUDGET` | 12% | `ENR-LAT-1`. Exceeding it aborts the v7 ship; the three pre-authorized mitigations are named in advance in that step. |
| `SKILL_MD_V6_GIT_SHA` | — | **`EV-2`**, the blob SHA of SKILL.md **immediately before `ENR-TAX-2`'s first byte.** ⚠ **`ENR-LAT-1` rebuilds the v6 prompt from `git show <sha>`; without it the v6 arm is unreproducible because `taxonomy_version()` has already moved.** |
| `SUBCATEGORY_RUBRIC_CHARS` | ~6,906 | The committed size of `SUBCATEGORIES.md`. Sets `ENR-PB-1`'s per-row amortization arithmetic and `ENR-PAR-1`'s "the rubric is shorter than SKILL.md" assertion. |
| `PARAM_CAT_DRAW_N` | 46 | **`EV-2b`.** Seeded, stratified proportional to the seed's draft distribution (**SWE 115→21, business_ops 56→10, data_scientist 48→9, product_manager 33→6**) with a floor of 5 per stratum. ⚠ **21 existing human + 46 = 67, clearing `GATE_MIN_N=50` with margin. `growth` and `hardware_engineer` have ZERO draft rows (both of each are already human), so no draw can raise their support.** Shrink to 35 only if the measured review rate is worse than ~40 s/row. |
| `PARAM_PRELABEL_ERROR_RATE` | — | ⚠ **`EV-2b`'s `category_audit.json`. There is NO prior for it today.** It is the empirical input that sets `PARAM_AUDIT_MAX` for the subcategory pass instead of the draft's invented 0.10. |
| `PARAM_AUDIT_MAX` | derived | `max(0.05, min(0.15, ceil(PARAM_PRELABEL_ERROR_RATE / 0.05) × 0.05))`. ⚠ **Above it, no `prelabel_agreed` row enters the gate pool and `EV-9b`'s stop condition fails.** |
| `PARAM_REGEX_COVERAGE` | — | **`EV-4`'s acceptance one-liner** over the 126 gold-SWE titles. ⚠ **The design track's 85/126 was measured against a 7-pattern regex over 13 slugs and does not survive the move to 15 (`ai_engineering` and `quantitative` added).** Sets tier A's size and therefore `EV-9b`'s review cost. |
| `PARAM_PAIRED_MIN_NET` | 4 at n≈67 | **`EV-5b` / `EV-12` G1b** — the minimum `b − c` that counts as a regression, ⚠ **paired with an exact-binomial p ≤ 0.05. BOTH conditions required so a 2-vs-0 flip (p=0.5) cannot fail the gate.** |
| `PARAM_MIN_CLASS_SUPPORT` | 5 | **`EV-5b`.** ⚠ **At the measured composition, `growth` and `hardware_engineer` sit at 2 rows each, where a single flipped row moves that class's F1 by 0.5 and the 6-class macro by 0.083 — four times the gate epsilon.** |
| `PARAM_CAL_DELTA` / `PARAM_NULL_RATE` | 5pp / 3% | **`EV-14`**, set against `EV-2`'s **OLLAMA** histogram. ⚠ **NEVER against the 71.8%/73.5% figures in circulation, which come from a 2026-07-08 claude-code artifact.** |
| G3's 0.65 primary-nonempty / 0.50 macro-F1 subcategory thresholds | — | ⚠ **Judgment calls across 15 classes, not derived numbers. The protocol doc must label them as opinion.** |
| `EV-13`'s backfill-lane acceptance floor | `G3_primary − 0.15` | ⚠ **Derived from the first MEASURED title+tags number, not invented; 0.15 is replaced once measured.** |
| The v6 and v7 full-seed run wall-clock | ~2 h per version at batch 4 | ⚠ **An ESTIMATE from 137.9 s/item, not a measurement. `EV-2` pilots 20 rows and publishes p50/p95 BEFORE committing to a schedule; the same number decides whether the A/B fits in one sitting.** |
| The two prod EXPLAIN numbers | — | **`RA-8`.** ⚠ **THRESHOLD IS PRE-COMMITTED: >250 ms or >20,000 buffers on either query ships `SCHEMA-12`; below both, do not ship it and record the numbers. Must run AFTER the backfill canary — an all-NULL column makes every plan look free.** |
| The measured request size for a 500-item `SCHEMA-14` batch | — | `BF-16` — the input to the drain-rate arithmetic. |
| `FacetTreeMultiSelect`'s `MenuProps.PaperProps.sx.maxHeight` | `{ xs: '60vh', sm: 400 }` | ⚠ **There is no maxHeight today and 6 parents + 15 children (21 rows) overflows.** Set by eyeballing the real menu at 1440px and at 375px once `FE-UI-1` mounts against real facets data. |
| The oracle fixture's row count N and its selection matrix | — | **Authored by `RA-4`.** `FE-ST-4`'s vitest half and `FE-CT-2`'s expansion-map assertion **both read whatever `RA-4` commits; neither invents rows.** ⚠ **It must cover null / `[]` / 1-slug / 2-slug / full_stack.** |
| The share of SWE rows that end at `subcategories: []` after the backfill | ~9% (~747 rows) | The actual backfill result. ⚠ **Governs how empty a narrow filter feels and how often `FE-CH-1`'s category-chip fallback branch renders in production.** |
| `VIRTUAL_LIST_CONFIG.ESTIMATED_CARD_HEIGHT` | unchanged | ⚠ **Do NOT adjust preemptively — card height is MEASURED by the virtualizer, so an extra chip is not a correctness risk; only scrollbar smoothness degrades.** Re-measure after the backfill establishes what share of cards carry two chips. |
| The literal pre-change cursor string `RA-5`'s no-churn test pins | — | ⚠ **Must be captured by RUNNING the current build against a fixed filter set — a wrong literal makes the test assert the opposite of what it is for.** |
| The post-#252 test-suite baseline (file count / test count) | — | ⚠ **One `npm test -- --run` on the merged branch BEFORE any of this work lands. #252 deletes eight frontend test files and adds six, so the draft's "157 files / 1,891 tests" is stale.** |
| `FE-CL-1.date` / `FE-CL-2.date` | — | The day PR-A merges / ⚠ **the day the reveal flag is FLIPPED, not the day the code lands.** |
| The reveal threshold (90% coverage) | 90% | Computed and DISPLAYED by `ADM-11`, ⚠ **flipped BY HAND. The frontend reads only a boolean and enforces nothing. Do NOT build auto-reveal — the record rejects it explicitly: a filter that switches itself on mid-session is surprising, and a threshold hovering near the line flaps the UI.** |
| `--until` for nights 2+, and whether a second night is needed at all | — | ⚠ **Read off `BF-10`'s morning report (rows remaining / measured seconds-per-row for the completed run), not a code constant.** |

## 5. Open risks that survive

1. ⚠ **The one-way door is a PROCESS risk, not a graph risk.** `ENR-TAX-2` depends on `EV-2` and `EV-2b`, **both of which are RUNS, not code.** The graph cannot stop someone bumping `version: v6` → `v7` before the v6 predictions are captured; once the hash moves, the v6 arm is only reproducible from `SKILL_MD_V6_GIT_SHA`. **If that SHA is not recorded, the A/B is unrecoverable.** `EV-2`'s acceptance is the only guard.

2. ⚠ **`EV-2b` needs ~25-40 minutes of Brendan's undivided review time, and `EV-9b` another 30-45.** Nothing in the plan can substitute for it — **promotion is deliberately DISABLED for the category pass because the pre-labeller and the draft labels are same-family.** The whole gate hangs on two blocks of human attention.

3. ⚠ **`growth` and `hardware_engineer` CANNOT be strengthened from the seed** — 2 rows each, both already human, zero draft rows. `EV-5b`'s support floor excludes them from the gated macro and reports them raw. **Raising their support needs `cli build-golden-set`, which must NOT be run mid-A/B** (it changes the row set the paired test pairs on, and until `EV-3` lands it also destroys four live seed keys). Named follow-up, not in scope.

4. ⚠ **ONE GATE CANNOT GOVERN TWO LANES.** The classify lane sees the compact §1b and a full description; the backfill lane sees the long rubric and title+tags. **Classify-lane subcategory precision will be lower than backfill-lane precision BY CONSTRUCTION**, and `subcategory_source` ('classify' vs 'backfill') is what lets you tell them apart after the fact. `EV-13` measures the second and is a **LOWER bound** until company tags reach the seed. **If only one arm is gated, the compact rubric ships unmeasured.**

5. ⚠ **The backfill's cheap layers read v6's own output as if it were an independent signal.** `_JOB_PROJECTION` (internal_enrichment.py:61-72) narrows `details` to `department` + `experience_level`, **so the enricher has NEVER received company tags — the `tags` in `enrichments` are the enricher's own v6 output.** `BF-6`'s row projection ships them to the model and `EV-4`'s tag layer scores against them. The disclosure is required in the module docstring, `P0-5`'s runbook and the morning report. Making them independent needs a one-line `COALESCE(details->'tags','[]'::jsonb)` on the projection — **unowned; named follow-up.**

6. ⚠ **`classify:engine` can still re-cross the injection boundary.** `BF-6` makes the boundary structural for the BACKFILL (the row dict has nowhere to put a description), **but the live Tier-1 path still sends full `clean_description` to whatever engine the knob names, and `claude-code` is a legal value (enrich_config.py:64). One knob flip puts thousands of untrusted scraped descriptions through headless `claude -p` — the exact thing `claude_code.py:8-10` says production must not do.** A validator on `set_classify_engine` is cheap. **Unowned.**

7. ⚠ **The starved 26 NULL-`first_seen_at` rows are now VISIBLE but UNREPAIRED.** Both candidate remedies — the deleted mutation and `ORDER BY COALESCE(first_seen_at, claimed_at)` — **change the live tick's claim order, which r11 declined. This needs an explicit yes from Brendan, not a migration side effect.**

8. ⚠ **Multi-category + subcategory is arguably surprising and the record does not address it.** Selecting Growth AND Backend yields `category=['growth','software_engineering'] AND subcategory=['backend']`, **which under AND composition hides every Growth row (they have no subcategories).** AND semantics were preserved rather than a special case invented; `renderValue` reads "Growth, Backend" so the state is at least legible. **Worth a product call before the reveal.**

9. ⚠ **Flag-off leak.** If a user sets a subcategory while the flag is ON and it is then flipped OFF, **the stored value keeps filtering invisibly — and post-#252 it also keeps going out on the wire as `?subcategory=`, because `buildSearchJobsArgs` is pure and deliberately does not read the flag.** Rare (the flag is one-way and `ADM-14` deletes it). **Documented rather than fixed**; a clobber-on-mount guard would destroy legitimately saved values.

10. ⚠ **MUI clone-element fragility.** Three undocumented `SelectInput` mechanics are load-bearing and all three re-verified at 7.3.6 (direct-children-only cloning; `child.props.onClick` firing before `setValueState`; `renderValue` short-circuiting). **A future MUI major could change any of them; `FE-UI-1`'s zero-`console.error` spy is what makes that fail loudly instead of quietly.**

11. ⚠ **The recency tiles will not narrow with a subcategory filter.** `SearchJobsCounts.total` tracks the active filter set and WILL reflect it, but `last24h`/`last3h` are company-scoped and scoped to nothing else by design (searchJobsTypes.ts:13-22). **That asymmetry predates this work, but a 15-way subdivision makes the gap between "Past 24 Hours" and the visible rows much more noticeable.** `FE-MG-1` documents it; **nobody has decided whether to change it.**

12. ⚠ **`ENR-TAX-2` changes the recorded hash twice in one commit** (the v6→v7 declared bump, and `taxonomy_version()` starting to fold `SUBCATEGORIES.md`). Both land atomically so there is no window, **but note the consequence: after v7, editing `SUBCATEGORIES.md` changes the hash and NOT the declared N, and `_version_below` (cli.py:1157-1180) treats same-N-different-hash as NOT below. Rubric-only edits will therefore NEVER be picked up by `reenrich` — which is the intended behaviour, but it must be written down or someone will expect otherwise.**

13. ⚠ **`P0-7`'s rehearsal is a point-in-time measurement of a GROWING database.** `MIGRATION_V3_V4_SECONDS` is measured on a 240 MB copy; **if the DB grows materially before the deploy, re-run it rather than trusting the recorded number against the 30 s split rule.**

14. ⚠ **`ENR-PB-1` is NEW code with no draft behind it.** It absorbs three symbols that were mutually shed, **so its content is reconstructed from `BF-2`'s and `ENG-3`'s descriptions plus the verified call sites, not from a written step anyone reviewed.** It is the highest-uncertainty step in PR-C, and `ENR-PAR-1` assertion 4 plus `EV-13` are the only things asserting it behaves.

15. ⚠ **`SCHEMA-14` has ONE client and no legacy fleet, which is why `extra='forbid'` is safe — but it also means nothing else exercises it.** If `BF-12b` drifts from the contract, **only `BF-12b`'s own respx tests catch it. The JVN-side round-trip in `GO-1` item 3 is the only cross-repo proof, and it must be run by hand.**

16. ⚠ **`ADM-8`'s own justification is struck, and the plan must not quietly restore it.** The admin correction dialog **cannot** reach `GATE_MIN_N=50` on its own: `merge_corrections` matches only the 273 seeded rows, the queue orders `enriched_at DESC` over 76k listings (~0.36% hit rate), and it produced 21 rows in a year. **D1 (`EV-2b`/`EV-9b`) is the only path to a real gate; the admin loop is SUSTAINING.** `GO-1` item 9 and `DEP-2` step 4 depend on D1 having run.

17. ⚠ **Nothing in JVN re-verifies the eval gate.** `eval/__init__.py`'s docstring and CLAUDE.md both claim the scorer "runs here, in the personal dir, and in JVN identically." **Verified FALSE — `JVN/src/backend/api/eval/scoring.py` is an unrelated location-normalization scorer and nothing in JVN imports `job_enricher.eval.scoring`.** `EV-17` corrects the docs; **any downstream plan assuming a second independent gate is relying on a mechanism that does not exist.**

---

## 6. Verification statement

I mechanically checked the final dependency graph before writing this document — **all 121 steps, every `dependsOn` id resolves to a step defined in this file, there are no cycles, no id appears in two PRs, every step appears in exactly one PR, and each PR's listed order is a valid topological order.**

The single cross-PR back-edge (`ENR-TAX-2` ← `EV-2` / `EV-2b`) is the deliberate one-way door and is called out as PR-C's two-stage merge, with PR-D stage 1 running in between.
